\lim_{l\rightarrow\infty}f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{t_{l}})=f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{o})
. It follows that

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\bar{{\mathbf{D}}}_{\varphi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})=0.

\mathcal{T}
is a well-defined function because of the strict convexity of the
g
-optimization problem. From the continuity assumptions,

0=\lim_{l\rightarrow+\infty}\bar{{\mathbf{D}}}_{\varphi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t_{l}+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t_{l})})=\bar{{\mathbf{D}}}_{\varphi}(\mathcal{T}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{o}),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{o})

and thus
\mathcal{T}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{o})=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{o}
, i.e.,
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{o}
is a fixed point of
\mathcal{T}
. ∎

A.10
Proof of Proposition
3

First, we show a result when using the Bregman surrogate
g(\boldsymbol{\beta};\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})=l(\boldsymbol{\beta})+P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})
for solving
\min_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f(\boldsymbol{\beta})=l(\boldsymbol{\beta})+P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta})
where
l
and
P
directionally differentiable and can be nonconvex. Define

\mathcal{L}_{P}:=\inf\{\mathcal{L}\in\mathbb{R}:\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}+\mathcal{L}\mathbf{D}_{2}\geq 0\},

(A.16)

which provides an index to characterize the degree of nonconvexity of
P
, c.f.
[
36
]
.
Assume
\mathcal{L}_{P}>-\infty
. Then for
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}\in\arg\min_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}g(\boldsymbol{\beta};\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})
, the following inequality holds for all
T\geq 1

\mathop{\mathrm{avg}}_{0\leq t\leq T}(2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{\psi}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}-\varrho^{2}\mathcal{L}_{P}\mathbf{D}_{2})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\leq\frac{1}{T+1}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(0)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)})\big].

The result can be proved from Theorem
2
, noticing the fact that

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})\geq\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})-\mathcal{L}_{P}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})-\varrho^{2}\mathcal{L}_{P}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})
for any
\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-}
. The details are omitted.

It suffices to proving the following lemma to complete the proof of Proposition
3
.

Lemma A.3
.

Given any thresholding function
\Theta
satisfying Definition
3
, let
P_{\Theta}
be the
\Theta
-induced penalty in (
48
). Then
\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}
as defined in (
47
) equals
\mathcal{L}_{P_{\Theta}}
that is given in (
A.16
).

Since
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P_{\Theta}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})=\sum_{j}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P_{\Theta}}(\beta_{j},\gamma_{j})
, it suffices to show the result in the univariate case.
Recall that
\Theta^{-1}(u;\lambda):=\sup\{t:\Theta(t;\lambda)\leq u\},\forall u>0
.
Since
P_{\Theta}(\gamma)=P_{\Theta}(|\gamma|)=\int_{0}^{|\gamma|}(\Theta^{-1}(u;\lambda)-u)\,\mathrm{d}u
, we assume
\gamma\geq 0
without loss of generality. We define
s(u;\lambda)=\Theta^{-1}(u;\lambda)-u
for
u\geq 0
, and extend
s(\cdot)
to
(-\infty,0)
by
s(-u)=-s(u),u>0
. Clearly,
s^{\prime}(u)=s^{\prime}(|u|)
a.e., and so
-\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}=\mathrm{ess\,inf}\{s^{\prime}(u;\lambda):u\neq 0\}
.
By definition,

\delta P_{\Theta}(\gamma;\beta-\gamma)=\begin{cases}s(\gamma)(\beta-\gamma),&\text{ if }\gamma\geq 0,\\
s(0)|\beta|,&\text{ if }\gamma=0.\end{cases}

When
\beta\geq 0
and
\gamma\neq 0
, we get

\begin{split}&\,(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P_{\Theta}}+\mathcal{L}\mathbf{D}_{2})(\beta,\gamma)\\
=&\,P_{\Theta}(\beta)-P_{\Theta}(\gamma)-\delta P_{\Theta}(\gamma;\beta-\gamma)+\mathcal{L}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\beta,\gamma)\\
=&\int_{\gamma}^{\beta}s(u)\,\mathrm{d}u-s(\gamma)(\beta-\gamma)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}(\beta-\gamma)^{2}\\
=&\int_{\gamma}^{\beta}s(u)\,\mathrm{d}u-\int_{\gamma}^{\beta}s(\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}u+\mathcal{L}\int_{\gamma}^{\beta}(u-\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
=&\int_{\gamma}^{\beta}\big[s(u)-s(\gamma)+\mathcal{L}(u-\gamma)\big]\,\mathrm{d}u\\
=&\int_{\gamma}^{\beta}\int_{\gamma}^{u}\big[s^{\prime}(v)+\mathcal{L}\big]\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}u.\end{split}

When
\beta<0
and
\gamma\neq 0
,

\begin{split}&\,(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P_{\Theta}}+\mathcal{L}\mathbf{D}_{2})(\beta,\gamma)\\
=&\,P_{\Theta}(\beta)-P_{\Theta}(\gamma)-\delta P_{\Theta}(\gamma;\beta-\gamma)+\mathcal{L}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\beta,\gamma)\\
=&\int_{\gamma}^{-\beta}s(u)\,\mathrm{d}u-s(\gamma)(\beta-\gamma)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}(\beta-\gamma)^{2}\\
=&\int_{-\gamma}^{-\beta}s(u)\,\mathrm{d}u-\int_{-\gamma}^{-\beta}s(-\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}u+\mathcal{L}\int_{-\gamma}^{-\beta}(u+\gamma)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
=&\int_{-\gamma}^{-\beta}\big[s(u)-s(-\gamma)+\mathcal{L}(u+\gamma)\big]\,\mathrm{d}u\\
=&\int_{-\gamma}^{-\beta}\int_{-\gamma}^{u}\big[s^{\prime}(v)+\mathcal{L}\big]\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}u.\end{split}

Similarly, when
\gamma=0
,
(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P_{\Theta}}+\mathcal{L}\mathbf{D}_{2})(\beta,0)=\int_{0}^{|\beta|}\int_{0}^{u}[s(v)+\mathcal{L}]\,\mathrm{d}v\,\mathrm{d}u
.
It is then easy to verify that
\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}=\mathcal{L}_{P_{\Theta}}
.
∎

A.11
Proof of Proposition
4

Let
f(\boldsymbol{\beta})=l(\boldsymbol{\beta})+P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta})
and recall

\begin{split}g^{(t)}_{\mathrm{LLA}}(\boldsymbol{\beta};\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})&=f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})\\
&=f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\sum_{j}(\alpha_{j}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P})(\varrho\beta_{j},\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t)}).\end{split}

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
2
and we give some details for completeness.
The important fact
\beta^{(t+1)}\in\mathop{\arg\min}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}g^{(t)}_{\mathrm{LLA}}(\boldsymbol{\beta};\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})
as shown in Example
5
implies

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{g^{(t)}_{\mathrm{LLA}}(\cdot;\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\leq f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})
or

\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
\geq\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\cdot,\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})\\
=\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\cdot,\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})\\
=\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}).\\
=\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}).\end{split}

The conclusion follows from summing up this inequality for
t=0,1,\ldots,T
.

A.12
Proofs of Theorem
3
and Theorem
4

Let
f(\boldsymbol{\beta})=l(\boldsymbol{\beta})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)
and recall
g(\boldsymbol{\beta};\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})=f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})
. We first introduce a lemma.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{F}
. Then for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we have the following inequality regardless of the specific form of
\psi

\begin{split}&(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}-\varrho^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathbf{D}_{2})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}};\lambda)\\
\leq\,&{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda).\end{split}

(A.17)

Denote
\hat{g}(\boldsymbol{\beta}):=g(\boldsymbol{\beta};\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})=l(\boldsymbol{\beta})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
.
Since
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
is a minimizer of
\hat{g}(\cdot)
, Lemma
A.1
shows that for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\hat{g}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\leq\hat{g}(\boldsymbol{\beta})-\hat{g}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
. On the one hand,

\displaystyle\,\hat{g}(\boldsymbol{\beta})-\hat{g}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,l(\boldsymbol{\beta})-l(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)-P_{\Theta}(\varrho\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}};\lambda)+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,l(\boldsymbol{\beta})-l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})-(l(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}))+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)-P_{\Theta}(\varrho\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}};\lambda)+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\langle\nabla l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}),\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\rangle-(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\langle\nabla l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}),\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\rangle)

\displaystyle\,+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)-P_{\Theta}(\varrho\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}};\lambda)+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)-P_{\Theta}(\varrho\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}};\lambda)+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}).

On the other hand, by Lemma
1
, Lemma
2
, and Lemma
A.3
,

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\hat{g}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})=&\,\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P_{\Theta}(\varrho\cdot)}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\cdot,\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\\
=&\,\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P_{\Theta}(\varrho\cdot)}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\\
\geq&\,\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\varrho^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}).\end{split}

The conclusion follows.
∎

To handle the stochastic term
\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle
in (
A.17
), we introduce the following result.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\boldsymbol{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
,
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}
be a sub-Gaussian random vector with mean 0 and scale bounded by
\sigma
, and
\lambda^{o}=\sigma\sqrt{\log(ep)}
. Suppose that
\varrho\geq\|\boldsymbol{X}\|_{2}
. Then there exist universal constants
A_{0},C,c>0
such that for any
a\geq 2b>0
and
A_{1}\geq A_{0}
, the following event

\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\Big\{2\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle-\frac{1}{a}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{b}[P_{H}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\sqrt{ab}{A_{1}\lambda^{o}})]\Big\}\geq a\sigma^{2}t

occurs with probability at most
C\exp(-ct)p^{-cA_{1}^{2}}
.

The lemma can be proved by Lemma 4 of
[
49
]
based on a scaling argument.

Let

R=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\{\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle-\frac{1}{2a}\|\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2b}[P_{H}(\varrho(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta});\sqrt{ab}{A\lambda^{o}})]\}

with
\lambda^{o}=\sigma\sqrt{\log(ep)}
.
Plugging the bound into (
A.17
) gives

\begin{split}&(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}-\varrho^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathbf{D}_{2})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}};\lambda)-P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)\\
\leq\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\frac{1}{2a}\|\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2b}[P_{H}(\varrho(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta});\sqrt{ab}{A\lambda^{o}})]+R\end{split}

(A.18)

with
\,\mathbb{P}(2R\geq a\sigma^{2}t)\leq C\exp(-ct)p^{-cA^{2}}
for any
a\geq 2b>0
and
A
large.

To prove Theorem
3
, substitute
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
for
\boldsymbol{\beta}
in (
A.18
) and combine it with the regularity condition (
57
), resulting in

\displaystyle\big(\delta-\frac{1}{a}\big)\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{X}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+\big(\vartheta-\frac{1}{2b}\big)P_{H}(\varrho(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*};\lambda)\leq K\lambda^{2}J(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+R

where
\lambda=\sqrt{ab}A\lambda^{o}
,
a\geq 2b>0
, and
A\geq A_{0}
with
A_{0}
given in Lemma
A.5
. Setting
a=2/(\delta\wedge(2\vartheta)),b=1/(2\vartheta)
or
a=2/((2\delta)\wedge\vartheta),b=1/\vartheta
bounds
{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})
or
P_{H}(\varrho(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*});\lambda)
. Finally, by Lemma
A.5
,
\mathbb{P}(R\leq 0)\geq 1-Cp^{-cA^{2}}
.

For Theorem
4
, we combine (
A.18
) and (
60
) with
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\big(\delta-\frac{1}{a}\big)\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\leq\,

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{L}r\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+K\lambda^{2}J(\boldsymbol{\beta})+R.

Take the same choice for
\lambda
and set
a=2/(\delta\wedge(2\vartheta)),b=1/(2\vartheta)
.

Case (i):
\alpha r/L=0
. The conclusion follows easily, and does not need any restriction on
\alpha
or
L
.

Case (ii):
\alpha r/L>0
. Then
0<r<1
and
\alpha/L>0
. If
\alpha<0
,
({\alpha}r/{L})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l_{0}}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\leq\alpha rd^{2}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\leq 0
, reducing to the first case. Assume
\alpha>0
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{L}r\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha}{L}r\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{X}\beta},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\leq\alpha rd^{2}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha r(d(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{X}\beta}^{*})+d(\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{X}\beta}^{*}))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha r(1+1/M)d^{2}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{X}\beta}^{*})+\alpha r(1+M)d^{2}(\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq r(1+1/M)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+r(1+M)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}),

for any
M>0
. Take
M=(1-r)/(1+r)
. Then

1/\{1-r(1+M)\}=1+r(1+1/M)=(1+r)/(1-r).

So we obtain

\displaystyle(\frac{1-r}{1+r}{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{l_{0}}+\frac{\delta}{2}{\mathbf{D}}_{2})(\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\

\displaystyle\frac{1+r}{1-r}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l_{0}}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\frac{KA^{2}}{((2\vartheta)\wedge\delta)\vartheta}\sigma^{2}J(\boldsymbol{\beta})\log(ep)+R.

Finally, from
\,\mathbb{P}(2R\geq a\sigma^{2}t)\leq C\exp(-ct)p^{-cA^{2}}\leq C\exp(-ct)
, we have
\,\mathbb{E}R\leq Ca\sigma^{2}
. The oracle inequality is proved. In fact, we also get

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})]\leq\frac{2(1+r)}{(1-r)\delta}\,\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l_{0}}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})]

\displaystyle+\frac{2KA^{2}}{(\vartheta\wedge\delta)\vartheta\delta}\sigma^{2}J(\boldsymbol{\beta})\log(ep)+\frac{C}{(\vartheta\wedge\delta)\delta}\sigma^{2}

under the same condition.

Remark A.1
.

Recall
J^{*}=J(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}),\mathcal{J}^{*}=\mathcal{J}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})
and
P(\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)=\sum_{j}P(\beta_{j};\lambda)
. When
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
is a global minimizer, applying the bound of the stochastic term proved in Lemma
A.5
gives the same conclusions (
58
), (
59
), under

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\vartheta P_{H}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*});\lambda)+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*};\lambda)\\
\leq\,&(2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{l_{0}}-\delta\mathbf{D}_{2})(\boldsymbol{X\beta},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)+K\lambda^{2}J^{*},\forall\boldsymbol{\beta}\end{split}

(A.19)

for some
\delta>0
,
\vartheta>0
and large enough
K\geq 0
. Assuming
P_{\Theta}
is subadditive, we can follow the arguments in Remark
2
to show that (
A.19
) is implied by
(1+\vartheta)P_{\Theta}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})_{\mathcal{J}^{*}};\lambda)\leq(2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{l_{0}}-\delta\mathbf{D}_{2})(\boldsymbol{X\beta},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*})+K\lambda^{2}J^{*}+(1-\vartheta)P_{\Theta}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})_{\mathcal{J}^{*c}};\lambda)
. Furthermore, when
l_{0}
is
\mu
-strongly convex as in regression, one can take
\delta=\mu
and the regularity condition is implied by

\displaystyle(1+\bar{\vartheta})P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathcal{J}^{*}};\lambda)\leq\bar{K}\lambda\sqrt{J^{*}}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{2}+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathcal{J}^{*c}};\lambda),\forall\boldsymbol{\gamma}

(A.20)

for some
\bar{\vartheta}\,(=\!\frac{2\vartheta}{1-\vartheta})>0
and
\bar{K}\big(\kern-4.0pt=\!\frac{\sqrt{2(2\mu-\delta)K}}{1-\vartheta}\big)\geq 0
, or the constrained forms

\displaystyle[P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathcal{J}^{*}};\lambda)]^{2}\leq\tilde{K}\lambda^{2}J^{*}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2},\ \,\forall\boldsymbol{\gamma}:P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathcal{J}^{*c}};\lambda)\leq(1+\bar{\vartheta})P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathcal{J}^{*}};\lambda)

(A.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}^{*}}P_{\Theta}^{2}(\varrho\gamma_{j};\lambda)\leq\tilde{K}\lambda^{2}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2},\ \,\forall\boldsymbol{\gamma}:P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathcal{J}^{*c}};\lambda)\leq(1+\bar{\vartheta})P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\gamma}_{\mathcal{J}^{*}};\lambda)

(A.22)

for some
\bar{\vartheta}>0
and
\tilde{K}\geq 0
. The conclusions and conditions can also be formulated in the oracle inequality setup of Theorem
4
. (
A.20
), (
A.21
) and (
A.22
)
extend the comparison condition (
62
), compatibility condition and RE condition to a more general penalty.

A.13
Proof of Theorem
5

We prove the result under a more relaxed assumption:
l
,
g
are merely directionally differentiable, and (
64
) is replaced by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(\varrho^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathbf{D}_{2}+\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta})+({\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi}-{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi}(\cdot;\boldsymbol{\alpha})})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta})+\vartheta P_{H}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*});\lambda)+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*};\lambda)\\
\leq\,&(2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{l_{0}}-\delta\mathbf{D}_{2})(\boldsymbol{X\beta},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)+K\lambda^{2}J(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}),\forall\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}\end{split}

for some
\delta>0
,
\varepsilon>0
,
\vartheta>0
and large
K\geq 0
.

Recall the objective function
f(\boldsymbol{\beta})=l(\boldsymbol{\beta})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)
and the surrogate function
g(\boldsymbol{\beta};\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})=f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})
. From Lemma
A.2
and Lemma
A.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})+(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi}(\cdot;\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P_{\Theta}(\varrho\cdot)})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})+(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi}(\cdot;\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}-\varrho^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathbf{D}_{2})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}).

Substituting
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
for
\boldsymbol{\beta}
yields

\begin{split}&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})\\
\geq\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})+(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi}(\cdot;\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
&+l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)};\lambda)-P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*};\lambda)\\
=\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi}(\cdot;\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
&2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*}\rangle+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)};\lambda)-P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*};\lambda).\end{split}

From the above regularity condition,

\begin{split}&\varepsilon\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\mathcal{L}_{\Theta}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\delta\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
&+\vartheta P_{H}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*});\lambda)+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta^{*}};\lambda)+(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi}(\cdot;\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
\leq\,&2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+P_{\Theta}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)};\lambda)+K\lambda^{2}J(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}),\end{split}

and so we obtain

\begin{split}&(\varepsilon+1)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})\\
&+\delta\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{(t+1)})+\vartheta P_{H}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*});\lambda)\\
\leq\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})+\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*}\rangle+K\lambda^{2}J^{*}.\end{split}

(A.23)

According to Lemma
A.5
, as long as
A\geq A_{0}/\sqrt{2}
, choosing
\lambda=\sqrt{2ab}A\lambda^{o}
,
b=1/(2\vartheta),a=1/(\delta\wedge\vartheta)
guarantees that the probability of the following inequality occurring for all
t
,

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*}\rangle-\delta\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{(t+1)})-\vartheta P_{H}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*});\lambda)\geq 0,

is no greater than
Cp^{-cA^{2}}
. Together with (
A.23
), with probability
1-Cp^{-cA^{2}}

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon+1}[\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})+K\lambda^{2}J^{*}]

(A.24)

for all
t
.
The desired inequality can be shown by iteratively applying (
A.24
) for
t=0,1,2,\ldots
.

A.14
Proofs of Theorem
6
and Corollary
2

First we prove Theorem
6
. Note that (
67b
), (
69b
) have additional terms involving
\mu_{0}
.
The first result for
\mu_{0}=0
can be shown based on a GBF translation of the proof of Proposition 1 in
[
57
]
.
For convenience, let
h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\allowbreak=f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})=f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\mu_{0}{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})
. Applying Lemma
A.2
to (
67b
) yields
(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}+\theta_{t}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})\leq h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\theta_{t}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})-\theta_{t}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})

\forall\boldsymbol{\beta}
, or

\begin{split}&h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})-h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\theta_{t}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})\\
\leq\,&\theta_{t}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-(\theta_{t}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)}).\end{split}

(A.25)

By definition,
\mathbf{C}_{h_{t}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t})=\theta_{t}h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})+(1-\theta_{t})h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})
; adding it to (
A.25
) multiplied by
\theta_{t}
gives

\begin{split}&h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-(1-\theta_{t})h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-\theta_{t}h_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\\
&+\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})+\mathbf{C}_{h_{t}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t})\\
&+\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\phi(\cdot)}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})\\
\leq\,&\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-(\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}+\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)}),\end{split}

and so

\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-(1-\theta_{t})[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})]+\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})\\
&+\theta_{t}\{(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}+\theta_{t}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\phi(\cdot)})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})\}+R_{t}\\
\leq\,&\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}(\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})),\forall t\geq 0\end{split}

(A.26)

where
R_{t}
is given by
\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\mathbf{C}_{f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t})
.

Under
\mu_{0}=0
, (
A.26
) implies that

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]-\frac{1-\theta_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]+\frac{\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})}{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}+\frac{R_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\\
\leq\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)}).\end{split}

(A.27)

Since in this case (
69b
) gives
({1-\theta_{t}})/{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}={1}/{\theta_{t-1}^{2}\rho_{t-1}}
for any
t\geq 1
, we obtain the first conclusion

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\theta_{T}^{2}\rho_{T}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]-\frac{1-\theta_{0}}{\theta_{0}^{2}\rho_{0}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(0)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})}{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}+\frac{R_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\Big)\\
\leq\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(0)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(T+1)}).\end{split}

On the other hand, given
\mu_{0}\geq 0
, (
A.26
) can be written as

\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-(1-\theta_{t})[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})]\\
&+R_{t}+\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})\\
&+\theta_{t}(\mu_{0}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})-\phi(\cdot)}+\theta_{t}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\phi(\cdot)})(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})\\
\leq\,&\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\theta_{t}^{2}(\rho_{t}+\frac{\mu_{0}}{\theta_{t}})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)}).\end{split}

(A.28)

Therefore, we have

\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\theta_{t}^{2}(\rho_{t}+\frac{\mu_{0}}{\theta_{t}})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})+\theta_{t}\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})+R_{t}\\
\leq\,&(1-\theta_{t})[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})]+\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)}),\forall t\geq 0\end{split}

and from (
69b
),

\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\theta_{t}^{2}(\rho_{t}+\frac{\mu_{0}}{\theta_{t}})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})+\theta_{t}\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})+R_{t}\\
\leq\,&(1-\theta_{t})[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\theta_{t-1}^{2}(\rho_{t-1}+\frac{\mu_{0}}{\theta_{t-1}})\Delta_{\phi}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})],\forall t\geq 1\end{split}

The second conclusion can be obtained by a recursive argument and
R_{T}+\theta_{T}\mathcal{E}_{T}(\boldsymbol{\beta})+(1-\theta_{T})(R_{T-1}+\theta_{T-1}\mathcal{E}_{T-1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}))+\cdots(1-\theta_{T})\cdots(1-\theta_{1})(R_{0}+\theta_{0}\mathcal{E}_{0}(\boldsymbol{\beta}))=\sum_{t=0}^{T}(\prod_{s=t+1}^{T}(1-\theta_{s}))(R_{t}+\theta_{t}\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
).

Remark A.2
.

With the ‘
=
’ in (
69b
) replaced by ‘
\leq
’, (
71
) still holds
when
{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\phi}\geq 0
(or
\phi
is convex), and (
70
) still holds
if we set
\boldsymbol{\beta}
to be a minimizer of
f
. But the equality form of (
69b
) makes our conclusions applicable to say the noise-free statistical truth
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
, which may not be a minimizer of the sample-based objective. The same comment applies to Theorem
7
.

Also, it is trivial to see that the conclusions extend to a varying sequence of
\mu_{t}
. (Concretely, the
\mu_{0}
in (
67b
), (
68
), and
\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
becomes
\mu_{t}
, and the
\mu_{0}
in (
69b
), (
71
) becomes
\mu_{t-1},\mu_{T}
, respectively.) One can add backtracking for
\mu_{t}
in the algorithm to further reduce its iteration complexity.

Finally, we prove Corollary
’
‣
A.14
which implies
Corollary
2
and applies to any convex
l_{0}
in (
A.29
) below. The proof is based on an accumulative
R_{t}
bound that can be derived in a more general setup; see (
A.32
) in Remark
A.3
.

Here, the optimization problem of interest in “variable screening” is

\displaystyle\min l(\boldsymbol{\beta})=l_{0}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta})\mbox{ s.t. }\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{0}\leq q,

(A.29)

to estimate the target
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
satisfying the strict inequality
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\|_{0}<q
. Take
\mu_{0}=0
,
\phi=\|\cdot\|_{2}^{2}/2
,
\psi_{0}(\boldsymbol{\beta})=l(\boldsymbol{\beta})-\mathcal{L}\phi(\boldsymbol{\beta})
for some
\mathcal{L}\geq 0
.

Given
l_{0}
,
\boldsymbol{X}
, and
s\leq p
, we extend the notion of
restricted isometry numbers
\rho_{+},\rho_{-}

[
12
]
:

\rho_{-}(s){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})\leq{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{l_{0}}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\gamma})\leq\rho_{+}(s){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}),\forall\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}:\|\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{0}\leq s.

(A.30)

(The dependence on
\boldsymbol{X}
and
l_{0}
is dropped for the sake of brevity.)

Corollary
2
’
.

Let
l_{0}
be any convex function and
q
be any nonnegative integer no more than
p
. As long as
r=q/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\|_{0}>1
, for any
\mathcal{L}\geq\rho_{+}(2q)/\sqrt{r}
, there must exist a universal
\rho_{t}=\rho_{0},\forall t
, e.g.,

\rho_{0}=(1-1/\sqrt{r})\rho_{+}(2q),

and thus
\theta_{t+1}=(\sqrt{\theta_{t}^{4}+4\theta_{t}^{2}}-\theta_{t}^{2})/2
, so that the accelerated iterative quantile-thresholding according to (
67a
)–(
67c
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)})-l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})}{\theta_{T}^{2}}+T\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{avg}}_{0\leq t\leq T}\frac{{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}{\theta_{t}}+\frac{\mathcal{L}(1-\theta_{T})}{\theta_{T}^{3}}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T)})

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(0)}),\mbox{ for all }T\geq 0,

or
l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)})-l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\min_{0\leq t\leq T}{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})\leq A/T^{2}
with
A
independent of
T
.

Seen from the error measure,
r
should be appropriately large (but cannot be too large from the perspective of statistical accuracy.)

To prove the corollary, we first introduce a useful result
[
50
, Lemma 9]
.

Lemma A.6
.

Given
q\leq p
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\Theta^{\#}(\boldsymbol{y};q)
is a globally optimal solution to
\min_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}l(\boldsymbol{\beta})=\|\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}/2
s.t.
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{0}\leq q
. Let
\mathcal{J}=\mathcal{J}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
,
\hat{\mathcal{J}}=\mathcal{J}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
and assume
J(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})=q
. Then, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
with
J(\boldsymbol{\beta})\leq s=q/r
and
r\geq 1
,

l(\boldsymbol{\beta})-l(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\geq\{1-\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{J},\hat{\mathcal{J}})\}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}),

where
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{J},\hat{\mathcal{J}})=(|\mathcal{J}\backslash\hat{\mathcal{J}}|/|\hat{\mathcal{J}}\backslash\mathcal{J}|)^{1/2}\leq(s/q)^{1/2}=r^{-1/2}
.

Set
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
in the previous proof, and apply, instead of Lemma
A.2
, Lemma
A.6
(where
\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)}\|_{0}=q
due to the no-tie-occurring assumption) to (
67b
). (
A.25
) is then replaced by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&l(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})-l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\theta_{t}\rho_{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})\\
\leq\,&\theta_{t}\rho_{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-(\theta_{t}\rho_{t}+\mathcal{L})(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)}).\end{split}

Accordingly, (
A.26
) becomes

\begin{split}&l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})-(1-\theta_{t})(l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-l(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}))+R_{t}\\
&+\theta_{t}\Big\{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\big[\mathcal{L}(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}})-\frac{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}{\sqrt{r}}\big]{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})\Big\}\\
\leq\,&\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}({\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})),\end{split}

where
R_{t}
is the same as before. Based on Lemma
1
, Lemma
2
, Lemma
5
(together with some results in its proof), (
A.30
), and the following facts

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)}=\theta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})=\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t-1})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t-1)}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)}),\forall t\geq 1

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)}=\theta_{t}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)}),\forall t\geq 0

we obtain for all
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle R_{t}\geq\,

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{2}(\rho_{t}+\mathcal{L}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l_{0}}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\{\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-(1-\theta_{t}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})\}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{2}(\rho_{t}+\mathcal{L}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l_{0}}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\{\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-(1-\theta_{t})\theta_{t}^{2}(1-\theta_{t-1})^{2}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t-1)})\}

\displaystyle\geq\,

\displaystyle\theta_{t}^{2}(\rho_{t}+\mathcal{L}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})-\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{+}(2q){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t})\{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t-1})^{2}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t-1)})\},

and
R_{t}\geq\theta_{t}^{2}\{\rho_{t}+\mathcal{L}-\rho_{+}(2q)\}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})
as
t=0
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{R_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\geq\,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{\rho_{t}+\mathcal{L}-\rho_{+}(2q)}{\rho_{t}}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})+\mathcal{L}\frac{1-\theta_{T}}{\theta_{T}\rho_{T}}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(T+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T)})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\Big\{\frac{1-\theta_{t}}{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}-\frac{(1-\theta_{t})^{2}(1-\theta_{t+1})}{\rho_{t+1}}\Big\}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{\rho_{t}+\mathcal{L}-\rho_{+}(2q)}{\rho_{t}}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})+\frac{\mathcal{L}(1-\theta_{T})}{\theta_{T}^{3}\rho_{T}}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T)})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\frac{1-\theta_{t}}{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}\Big\{1-(1-\theta_{t})\theta_{t+1}\frac{\theta_{t+1}}{\theta_{t}}\Big\}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}).

Therefore, choosing a universal
\rho_{t}=\rho_{0}\geq\rho_{+}(2q)-\mathcal{L}
(which implies
\theta_{t}\downarrow
) ensures

\sum_{t=0}^{T}{R_{t}}/({\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}})\geq 0
.

Moreover,
\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})\geq\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})
holds
under
\big\{\mathcal{L}(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}})-\frac{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}{\sqrt{r}}\big\}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})\}\geq 0
or
L(1-{1}/{\sqrt{r}})\geq{\rho_{t}}/\sqrt{r}
.
It is easy to see that as long as
r>1
, there exist positive
\mathcal{L},\rho_{0}
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}(\sqrt{r}-1)\geq\rho_{0}\\
\rho_{0}\geq\rho_{+}(2q)-\mathcal{L}.\end{cases}

(A.31)

Furthermore, for any
\mathcal{L}\geq\rho_{+}(2q)/\sqrt{r}
, we can always choose
\rho_{0}=(1-1/\sqrt{r})\rho_{+}(2q)
.
The rest of the proof proceeds as before.

Remark A.3
.

The idea of controlling the overall
\sum_{t\leq T}\frac{R_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}
can be extended with a proper choice of
\psi_{0}
to a general problem
\min f(\boldsymbol{\beta})
that may be nonconvex. In fact, if
f(\boldsymbol{\beta})
can be decomposed as
l(\boldsymbol{\beta})+P(\boldsymbol{\beta})
with
0\leq{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{l}\leq L{\mathbf{D}}_{2}
and
{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{P}+\mathcal{L}_{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}\geq 0
for some finite
\mathcal{L}_{0}\geq 0
, then setting
\mu_{0}=0
,
\psi_{0}=l-\mathcal{L}\|\cdot\|_{2}^{2}/2
with
\mathcal{L}\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}
and repeating the previous arguments, we obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{R_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}&\geq\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{1}{\rho_{t}}(\rho_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\phi}+\mathcal{L}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}-L{\mathbf{D}}_{2})(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)})\\
+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}&\frac{1}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t+1)})+\frac{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}_{0})(1-\theta_{T})}{\theta_{T}^{3}\rho_{T}}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T)})\\
+\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}&\mathcal{L}\frac{1-\theta_{t}}{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}\Big\{\frac{\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}_{0}}{\mathcal{L}}-(1-\theta_{t})\theta_{t+1}\frac{\theta_{t+1}}{\theta_{t}}\Big\}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}).\end{split}

(A.32)

With
\rho_{t}=\rho_{0}
, it can be shown that

(1-\theta_{t})\theta_{t+1}\frac{\theta_{t+1}}{\theta_{t}}
achieves the maximum value
0.1612
at
t=3
and so
\mathcal{L}\geq 1.2\mathcal{L}_{0}
makes the last term nonnegative. (A varying
\rho_{t}
may reduce
\mathcal{L}
further.) Therefore, under
{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\phi}\geq\sigma{\mathbf{D}}_{2}
, we can choose any
\rho_{0}\geq(L-\mathcal{L})/\sigma
and
\mathcal{L}\geq 1.2\mathcal{L}_{0}
so that for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
,

\displaystyle\frac{f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})}{\theta_{T}^{2}}+T\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{avg}}_{0\leq t\leq T}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})}{\theta_{t}}+\frac{(\mathcal{L}-\mathcal{L}_{0})(1-\theta_{T})}{\theta_{T}^{3}}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T)})

\displaystyle\leq\rho_{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(0)}),\forall T\geq 0.

Hence for some
A
independent of
T
,

f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\min_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\leq A/T^{2}
, or
f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\min_{0\leq t\leq T}\{{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\psi_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\allowbreak\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t+1)})\}\leq A/T^{2}
when
\phi
is differentiable.

A.15
Proof of Theorem
7

The construction of the new acceleration scheme and the proof are motivated by Proposition 2 of
[
57
]
, with the use of GBF calculus. First, from Lemma
A.2
, given any
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t}
,

\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t})+(\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
\leq&(\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})-(\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\end{split}

(A.33)

Let
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t}=\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}
with
\theta_{t}
to be determined. Define
h_{t}(\cdot)=f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})
. By the definition of
\mathbf{C}
,

\begin{split}-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t})=\,&\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})\\
&-\theta_{t}f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-(1-\theta_{t})f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})+\mathbf{C}_{h_{t}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t}).\end{split}

Plugging the last equality into (
A.33
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-(1-\theta_{t})(f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}))\\
&+(\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\mathbf{C}_{h_{t}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t})\\
&+\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})\\
\leq\,&(\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})-(\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
&+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
=\,&\rho_{t}[\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})-\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})]-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{h_{t}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\end{split}

and based on the definition of
\bar{\psi}_{0}
and
R_{t}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+R_{t}\\
&+\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\mathbf{C}_{\mu_{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t})+\mu_{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
&+\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\bar{\psi}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})+\mathbf{C}_{f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f(\cdot)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\psi_{0}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
\leq\,&(1-\theta_{t})(f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}))+\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)}).\end{split}

From Section
A.5
,
\mathbf{C}_{\mu_{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t})=\mu_{0}\mathbf{C}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\theta_{t})=\mu_{0}\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})
and so

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+R_{t}+\theta_{t}\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\\
&+(\rho_{t}+\mu_{0})\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\mu_{0}\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})\\
\leq\,&(1-\theta_{t})(f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}))+\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}_{t},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)}).\end{split}

(A.34)

We would like to write
(\rho_{t}+\mu_{0})\mathbf{D}_{2}(\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\mu_{0}\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})
into the form of a multiple of
\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t+1)})
for some
\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t+1)}
. This can be done by solving the gradient equation with respect to
\boldsymbol{\beta}
:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t+1)}=\frac{(\rho_{t}+\mu_{0})\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}-\rho_{t}\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t}){\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{(t)}}{\rho_{t}\theta_{t}^{2}+\mu_{0}\theta_{t}}.

(A.35)

On the other hand,
\nabla\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\theta_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}+(1-\theta_{t})\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)})=\boldsymbol{0}

gives

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t)}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)}}{\theta_{t}}-\frac{1-\theta_{t}}{\theta_{t}}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}.

(A.36)

Combining (
A.35
) and (
A.36
) results in

\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}+\frac{\rho_{t-1}\theta_{t}(1-\theta_{t-1})}{\rho_{t-1}\theta_{t-1}+\mu_{0}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t-1)}),

(A.37)

as in (
76a
). Therefore,
(
A.34
) becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+(\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}+\mu_{0}\theta_{t})\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t+1)})+R_{t}+\theta_{t}\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\\
\leq\,&(1-\theta_{t})(f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}))+\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t)}).\end{split}

(A.38)

Let
\mu_{0}=0
. It follows from (
A.38
) that

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]+\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t+1)})+\frac{\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})}{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}+\frac{R_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\\
\leq\,&\frac{(1-\theta_{t})}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]+\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t)}),\forall t\geq 0.\end{split}

(A.39)

Under (
77b
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]+\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t+1)})+\frac{\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})}{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}+\frac{R_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\\
\leq\,&\frac{1}{\theta_{t-1}^{2}\rho_{t-1}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]+\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t)}),~~\forall t\geq 1.\end{split}

(A.40)

Summing (
A.40
) for
t=T,\ldots,1
and (
A.39
) for
t=0
gives

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\theta_{T}^{2}\rho_{T}}[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})]+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\Big(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})}{\theta_{t}\rho_{t}}+\frac{R_{t}}{\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}}\Big)\\
\leq\,&\frac{1-\theta_{0}}{\theta_{0}^{2}\rho_{0}}\big[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(0)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\big]+\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(0)})-\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(T+1)}),\end{split}

and so the first bound noticing that
\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(0)}=\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(0)}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(0)}
.

Moreover, given any
\mu_{0}\geq 0
, from (
77b
), (
A.38
) implies for any
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+(\theta_{t}^{2}\rho_{t}+\mu_{0}\theta_{t})\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t+1)})+R_{t}+\theta_{t}\mathcal{E}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta})\\
\leq\,&(1-\theta_{t})[f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta})+(\theta_{t-1}^{2}\rho_{t-1}+\mu_{0}\theta_{t-1})\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\nu}^{(t)})].\end{split}

(A.41)

Similar to the proof of Theorem
6
, a recursive argument using (
A.41
) and (
A.38
) gives the second bound.

Remark A.4
.

Compared with the proof of Theorem
6
, the proof here needs to perform a finer analysis of
\mathbf{C}_{h_{t}}
(the proof of Corollary
’
‣
A.14
uses a similar treatment). Otherwise one would get
\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{(t)}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}+\theta_{t}(\theta_{t-1}^{-1}-1)(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t-1)})
and
\rho_{t}{\theta_{t}^{2}}/({1-\theta_{t}})={\theta_{t-1}^{2}(\rho_{t-1}+\mu_{0})}

in place of (
76a
), (
77b
), respectively. Following the same proof, we can show that the resultant algorithm does result in a linear rate when
\mu_{0}=\mu>0
, but offers no acceleration (
\theta_{0}=1/\kappa
) in strongly smooth and convex optimization.

Finally, Remark
A.2
still applies. For example, the second conclusion holds when the ‘
=
’ in (
77b
) is replaced by ‘
\leq
’, and it is straightforward to see that
\mu_{0}
can be similarly replaced by a sequence of varying
\mu_{t}
to speed the convergence.

A.16
Statistical accuracy of LLA iterates

In this subsection, assume
f(\boldsymbol{\beta})=l(\boldsymbol{\beta})+P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta})
,
l(\boldsymbol{\beta})=l_{0}(\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta})
,
P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta})=\sum_{j}P(\varrho\beta_{j})
(by a slight abuse of notation),
P(0)=0
,
P^{\prime}_{+}(0)<+\infty
,
P(t)=P(-t)\geq 0
,
P(t)
is differentiable for any
t>0
, and
P
is concave on
(0,+\infty)
. Recall
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{LLA}}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)}\text{$\circ$}(\cdot)\|_{1}-P(\cdot)}
which does
not
satisfy the strong idempotence.

Assumption

\mathcal{A}(\varepsilon,\delta,\vartheta,K,\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta})
Given
\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}
, there exist
\varepsilon>0,\delta>0,\vartheta>0,K\geq 0
such that the following inequality holds

\displaystyle(1+\varepsilon)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\text{$\circ$}(\cdot)\|_{1}-P(\cdot)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta})+\delta\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X\beta})+\vartheta P_{H}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*});\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta})+P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta};\lambda)-P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*};\lambda)+K\lambda^{2}J^{*}.

Proposition 5
.

Assume that for any given
T\geq 1
,
\mathcal{A}(\varepsilon,\delta,\vartheta,K,\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})
(
1\leq t\leq T
) is satisfied for some
\varepsilon>0,\delta>0,\vartheta>0,K\geq 0
. Let
\lambda=A\sigma\sqrt{\log(ep)}/\sqrt{(\delta\wedge\vartheta)\vartheta}
.
Then the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-Cp^{-cA^{2}}

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(T)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(T)})\leq\kappa^{T}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(0)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(0)})+\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}K\lambda^{2}J^{*},

where
\kappa=1/(1+\varepsilon)
and
C,c
are universal positive constants.

From the proof of Proposition
4
, for any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
,

\begin{split}&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\cdot,\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
\leq\,&f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}).\end{split}

Using the definition of
\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{LLA}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P(\cdot,\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})\\
&+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\cdot,\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t)})}(\varrho\beta_{j},\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t+1)})\\
\leq\,&f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}),\end{split}

(A.42)

where we used
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})\geq 0
since
P(\cdot)
is concave on
(0,+\infty)
.

Lemma A.7
.

For any
P(\cdot)
which is differentiable on
(0,+\infty)
and satisfies
P(t)=P(-t)\geq 0,P(0)=0
and
P^{\prime}_{+}(0)<+\infty
, we have
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\cdot,\alpha)}(\beta,\gamma)=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\beta,\gamma)-P^{\prime}_{+}(0)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\beta,\allowbreak\gamma)1_{\alpha=0}
for any
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
.
In particular,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\cdot,\alpha)}(\beta,\gamma)=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\beta,\gamma)1_{\alpha\neq 0}
.

The result can be shown from the proof of Lemma
2
. Indeed, from (
29
),

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\cdot,\alpha)=\begin{cases}P(\cdot)-P(\alpha)-P^{\prime}(\alpha)(\cdot-\alpha),&\alpha\neq 0\\
P(\cdot)-P^{\prime}_{+}(0)|\cdot|,&\alpha=0.\end{cases}

When
\alpha\neq 0
, by Lemma
1
and Lemma
2
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\cdot,\alpha)}(\beta,\gamma)=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\beta,\gamma)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P(\alpha)+P^{\prime}(\alpha)(\cdot-\alpha)}(\beta,\gamma)\allowbreak=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\beta,\gamma)
.
When
\alpha=0
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\cdot,\alpha)}(\beta,\gamma)=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\beta,\gamma)-P^{\prime}_{+}(0)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\beta,\gamma)
.
Combining the two cases gives

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\cdot,\alpha)}(\beta,\gamma)=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\beta,\gamma)-P^{\prime}_{+}(0)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\beta,\gamma)1_{\alpha=0}.

When
P(\boldsymbol{\beta})=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\cdot,\alpha)}(\beta,\gamma)=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\beta,\gamma)-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\beta,\gamma)1_{\alpha=0}=\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\beta,\gamma)1_{\alpha\neq 0}
.

From Lemma
A.7
,

\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})-\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{P(\cdot,\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})}}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})=\sum_{j:\beta_{j}^{(t)}=0}P^{\prime}_{+}(0)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\varrho\beta_{j},\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t+1)})

and

\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\cdot,\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t)})}(\varrho\beta_{j},\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t+1)})=\sum_{j:\beta_{j}^{(t)}\neq 0}\alpha_{j}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\varrho\beta_{j},\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t+1)}).

Plugging these into
(
A.42
) gives
\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\sum_{j:\beta_{j}^{(t)}=0}P^{\prime}_{+}(0)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\varrho\beta_{j},\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t+1)})+\sum_{j:\beta_{j}^{(t)}\neq 0}\allowbreak\alpha_{j}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\varrho\beta_{j},\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t+1)})\leq f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})
.

Together with
\alpha_{j}^{(t)}=|P^{\prime}_{+}(\beta_{j}^{(t)})|\leq P^{\prime}_{+}(0)
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}^{(t)}\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{1}(\varrho\beta_{j},\varrho\beta_{j}^{(t+1)})\leq f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{LLA}}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)}).

(A.43)

Letting
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
and using the definition of
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}
,
we obtain

\begin{split}&2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)}\text{$\circ$}(\cdot)\|_{1}}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)};\lambda)\\
\leq\,&\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{(t)}_{\mathrm{LLA}}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t)})+\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\rangle+P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*};\lambda).\end{split}

From the regularity condition,

\displaystyle(1+\varepsilon)\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\mathrm{LLA}}^{(t+1)}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\delta\mathbf{D}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{X\beta}^{(t+1)})\allowbreak+\vartheta P_{H}(\varrho(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*});\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\bar{\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{(t)}\text{$\circ$}(\cdot)\|_{1}}(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)})+P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(t+1)};\lambda)-P(\varrho\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*};\lambda)+K\lambda^{2}J^{*}

for
1\leq t\leq T
.
The final conclusion can be proved by combining the last two inequalities and then applying a similar probabilistic argument as in Theorem
5
.
∎

A.17
A-estimators as F-estimators

In this part, we show that an important class of
A
-estimators that has
alternative
optimality, typically arising from block coordinate descent (BCD) algorithms, can often be converted to F-estimators, and analyzed in a similar way. Let
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[1]}^{T},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[K]}^{T}]^{T}
where
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]}
is the
k
th block,
1\leq k\leq K
, and we use
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[-k]}
to denote the subvector after removing the
k
th block. Assume

f=l+P

where
l
is differentiable, and
P
is separable:
P(\boldsymbol{\beta})=\sum P_{k}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]})
. When viewed as a function of
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]}
only,
f
is denoted by
f(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]};\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[-k]})
. We say
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
has alternative optimality or is an A-estimator if

\hat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{[k]}\in\arg\min_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]}}f({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]};\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[-k]}),1\leq k\leq K.

(A.44)

Lemma A.8
.

Let
\hat{\beta}
be an A-estimator of
\min f(\boldsymbol{\beta})
. Construct a surrogate function:

\displaystyle g_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\beta};\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})=f(\boldsymbol{\beta})-{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{l}(\boldsymbol{\beta},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})+\sum\rho_{k}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]}^{-})

(A.45)

where
\boldsymbol{\rho}=(\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{K})
with
\rho_{k}\geq 0
.

(i) If
P_{k}
are directionally differentiable and

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{P_{k}}+\mathcal{L}_{k}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}\geq 0

(A.46)

for some
\mathcal{L}_{k}\geq 0
, then for any
\rho_{k}\geq\mathcal{L}_{k}
,
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
must satisfy

\hat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}\in\arg\min_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}g_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\beta};\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-})|_{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}.

(A.47)

(ii) If
l
as a function of
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{k}
satisfies
{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{l(\cdot;\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[-k]})}\leq L_{k}{\mathbf{D}}_{2},\forall\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[-k]}
,
1\leq k\leq K
, or less restrictively,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\hat{l}_{k}(\cdot)}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]})\leq L_{k}{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]}),\ \forall\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]}

(A.48)

where
\hat{l}_{k}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]})
denotes
l_{k}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]};\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[-k]})
, then for any
\rho_{k}>L_{k}
, (
A.47
) still holds.
In addition, if
\hat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}_{[k]}
is the unique solution to (
A.44
), then
\rho_{k}\geq L_{k}
suffices.

Overall, (
A.47
) provides a useful
joint
optimization form that can be used as the so-called “basic inequality” in empirical process theory, and so with the lemma, A-estimators can analyzed like F-estimators. Moreover, the quality of the initial point can be incorporated in the analysis; see
[
54
]
.

(i) The condition (
A.46
) means that
g_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
is convex in,
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]}
. By Lemma
4
and Lemma
1
, and the fact that
g_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
is separable in
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{[k]}
,
1\leq k\leq K
, we immediately know that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
is necessarily a solution to
\min_{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}g_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\beta};\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
.

(ii) We use a shorthand notation
\hat{g}_{k}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]})
to denote
g_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}(\boldsymbol{\beta};\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
as a function of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]}
when
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[-k]}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[-k]}
. Let
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]}\in\arg\min\hat{g}_{k}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]})
. It suffices to show
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]}=\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]}
. Because of the separability of
g_{\boldsymbol{\rho}}
,

f(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]};\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[-k]})+(\rho_{k}-L_{k}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]})\leq f(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]};\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[-k]})

and so
(\rho_{k}-L_{k}){\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{[k]})=0
. The conclusion follows.
∎

Some conclusions like (i) can be extended to functions defined on Riemannian manifolds. It is also worth mentioning that
in the regression setup, which is of primary interest in many statistical applications, we can use some surrogates with
\rho_{k}=1
, regardless of the design or penalty, to convert alternative optimality to joint optimality. The following lemma exemplifies the point in matrix regression, and is condition free.

Lemma A.9
.

Let
l_{0}(\boldsymbol{A};\boldsymbol{Y})=\|\boldsymbol{Y}-\boldsymbol{A}\|_{F}^{2}/2
, and
\boldsymbol{A}
be defined differently as follows.

(i) Let
\boldsymbol{A}=\sum\boldsymbol{X}_{k}\boldsymbol{B}_{k}
with
\boldsymbol{B}=(\boldsymbol{B}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{B}_{K})
, where the dependence of
\boldsymbol{B}
(and
\boldsymbol{X}_{k}
) is dropped for simplicity. Consider the problem

\displaystyle\min_{\boldsymbol{B}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{B}_{K}}l_{0}(\boldsymbol{A};\boldsymbol{Y})+\sum P_{k}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k})\mbox{ s.t. }\boldsymbol{A}=\sum\boldsymbol{X}_{k}\boldsymbol{B}_{k}.

(A.49)

Then the set of A-estimators of (
A.49
) is exactly the set of F-estimators associated with the following surrogate

\displaystyle g(\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{-})=l_{0}(\boldsymbol{A};\boldsymbol{Y})-{\mathbf{D}}_{l_{0}}(\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{A}^{-})+\sum P_{k}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k})+\sum{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\boldsymbol{X}_{k}\boldsymbol{B}_{k},\boldsymbol{X}_{k}\boldsymbol{B}_{k}^{-}).

(A.50)

(ii) Let
\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{B}_{1}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{K}
with
\boldsymbol{B}=(\boldsymbol{B}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{B}_{K})
, where the dependence of
\boldsymbol{B}
(and
\boldsymbol{X}_{k}
) is dropped for simplicity. Consider

\displaystyle\min_{\boldsymbol{B}_{1},\ldots,\boldsymbol{B}_{K}}l_{0}(\boldsymbol{A};\boldsymbol{Y})+\sum P_{k}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k})\mbox{ s.t. }\boldsymbol{A}=\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{B}_{1}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{K}.

(A.51)

Redefine
l_{0}
as a function
\bar{l}_{0}
of
\boldsymbol{B}
and introduce a discrepancy measure
d^{2}
as follows

\displaystyle l_{0}(\boldsymbol{A};\boldsymbol{Y})=\bar{l}_{0}(\boldsymbol{B};\boldsymbol{X},\boldsymbol{Y})

\displaystyle d^{2}(\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{-})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{B}_{1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{k-1}^{-}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k}-\boldsymbol{B}_{k}^{-})\boldsymbol{B}_{k+1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{K}^{-}\|_{F}^{2}.

Then the set of A-estimators of (
A.51
) is exactly the set of F-estimators associated with the surrogate

\displaystyle\begin{split}g(\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{-})&=\bar{l}_{0}(\boldsymbol{B})-{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{\bar{l}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{-})+\sum P_{k}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k})+d^{2}(\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{-})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{A}^{-}-\boldsymbol{Y}\|_{F}^{2}+\langle\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{B}_{1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{K}^{-}-\boldsymbol{Y},\\
&\quad\sum_{k=1}^{K}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{B}_{1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{k-1}^{-}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k}-\boldsymbol{B}_{k}^{-})\boldsymbol{B}_{k+1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{K}^{-}\rangle+\sum P_{k}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k})\\
&\quad\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{B}_{1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{k-1}^{-}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k}-\boldsymbol{B}_{k}^{-})\boldsymbol{B}_{k+1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{K}^{-}\|_{F}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.52)

The lemma can be directly proved by the definition of GBF and matrix differentiation and its proof is omitted.
For the application of the first result (i), see
[
50
]
for example. The second result can be used to study bilinear problems or NMF like matrix decomposition problems. One could show a statistical accuracy result in terms of
d^{1}
(which satisfies
d^{1}\leq Kd^{2}
),

d^{1}(\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{B}^{-})=\frac{1}{2}\big\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\boldsymbol{X}\boldsymbol{B}_{1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{k-1}^{-}(\boldsymbol{B}_{k}-\boldsymbol{B}_{k}^{-})\boldsymbol{B}_{k+1}^{-}\cdots\boldsymbol{B}_{K}^{-}\big\|_{F}^{2},

under a proper regularity condition involving
d^{2}
; see, for example,
[
53
]
.

A.18
Statistical error analysis of a general optimal solution

This part demonstrates that using the statistical notions and Bregman calculus developed earlier can perform statistical analysis of a general optimization problem that may not be in the MLE setup:

\displaystyle\min_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}f(\boldsymbol{\beta})\mbox{ s.t. }\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{S}

(A.53)

where
f
is directionally differentiable and

\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
can be formulated by linear equality constraints
\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, sparsity constraints
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{0}\leq s
, nonnegativity constraints
\boldsymbol{\beta}\geq 0
, and so on.

Statistically, we would like to study how a target parameter can be recovered from solving (
A.53
) in the present of data noise. Following (
53
), let
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
be a statistical truth and define the associated effective noise by
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}=-\nabla f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})
, assuming
f
is differentiable at
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
.

Although
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
in the above definition can be any point, a meaningful recovery must be under some conditions satisfied the associated
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}
. Consider the following three scenarios:

(a)
Statistical estimation often assumes a zero mean noise:

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}=\boldsymbol{0},

(A.54)

which essentially means that the statistical truth makes the gradient of the
expectation
of
f
(so as to remove data randomness) vanish—see Section
3.2
. Yet (
A.54
) alone does not always guarantee a unique
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
.

(b) Stronger conclusions can be obtained for the
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
that satisfies the no-model-ambiguity assumption:
f
is differentiable at
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\in D=\mbox{dom}(f)
with the gradient
\nabla f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})=-\boldsymbol{\epsilon}
,
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}
is a finite optimal solution to the Fenchel conjugate as
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=-\boldsymbol{\epsilon}
:

\displaystyle f^{*}(\boldsymbol{\zeta})=\sup_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\langle\boldsymbol{\zeta},\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle-f(\boldsymbol{\beta}),

(A.55)

and the extended real-valued convex function
f^{*}
is differentiable at
-\boldsymbol{\epsilon}
. This assumption simply means that
(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},-\boldsymbol{\epsilon})
makes a so-called “conjugate pair”. Note that
f
need not be overall strictly convex, especially when
D
is compact according to Danskin’s min-max theorem
[
6
]
.

(c) Another popular assumption in statistical learning is strong convexity in a restricted sense (especially when
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{A}\boldsymbol{\alpha}
with
\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{0}\leq s
):

\displaystyle({\boldsymbol{\Delta}}_{f}-\mu{\mathbf{D}}_{2})(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2})\geq 0,\ \forall\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{2}\in\mathcal{S}

(A.56)

for some
\mu>0
.
The condition may hold even when the number of unknowns is much larger than the sample size
[
12
,
36
]
.

The following theorem uses the GBF calculus to argue how the statistical accuracy of the obtained solutions is determined by the (tail decay of) effective noise. Probabilistic arguments can follow to bound the stochastic terms more explicitly.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
be an optimal solution to (
A.53
).

(i) Under the zero mean assumption (
A.54
) and
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\in\mathcal{S}
, the risk of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
in terms of
{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f}
satisfies a Fenchel-Young form bound

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}})\leq\,\mathbb{E}[f^{*}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon})+f(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})].

(A.57)

(ii) Under the no-model-ambiguity assumption in (b) and
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}})\leq{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\epsilon},-\boldsymbol{\epsilon}).

(A.58)

(iii) An oracle inequality holds for any
\delta>0
and any reference
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathcal{S}
:

\displaystyle({\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f}-\delta{\mathbf{D}}_{2})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\leq{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f}({\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})+\frac{1}{2\delta}[\,\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Gamma(\boldsymbol{\beta})}\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\theta}\rangle]^{2},

(A.59)

where

\Gamma(\boldsymbol{\beta})=\{\boldsymbol{\theta}:\|\boldsymbol{\theta}\|_{2}\leq 1,\boldsymbol{\theta}=\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-\boldsymbol{\beta}\mbox{ for some }\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathcal{S}\}
.
In particular, under (
A.56
),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}_{2}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})\leq\frac{1}{\mu}{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f}({\boldsymbol{\beta}},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*})+\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}[\,\sup_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\Gamma(\boldsymbol{\beta})}\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\boldsymbol{\theta}\rangle]^{2}.

(A.60)

The first two bounds reveal the important role of the Fenchel conjugate of the loss, and can be made more explicit under proper Orlicz norm conditions of
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}
; the third conclusion, on the basis of the supremum of an empirical process
[
60
]
, demonstrates how modern probabilistic tools can be used to derive finite-sample error bounds of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
in a general noisy setup.

First, by definition,
f(\boldsymbol{}\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\leq f({\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}})
, from which it follows that
{\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}})\leq\langle\boldsymbol{\epsilon},\hat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}}\rangle
. Define

h(\boldsymbol{\delta})={\mathbf{\Delta}}_{f}(\boldsymbol{\delta}+\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}).

By assumption,
f
is a proper function and applying Fenchel-Young’s inequality gives
